The root cause: `run ["sh" "-c" "printf '%s' '${out}' | grep …"]` interpolates the selftest output into a shell single-quoted string, so any apostrophe in the output (`cursor's`, `paint's`) closes the quote early and mangles the printf. The fix routes the output through no shell at all — Rishi's native `str contains "needle"` tests a Rishi value, so an apostrophe in the haystack can never break a check. Both HUNK48/49 witnesses converted from the printf-grep form to `assert out contains "…"`, and the HUNK49 selftest's natural apostrophe (`cursor's applied prefix`) **restored** to prove the loom on metal — the witness now passes *with* the apostrophe present, where the old form failed. Booked REDS #76 (row 75 was the stamp-fabrication red) with all three fields, tightened to keep `REDS.md` under its `living_pin_max_bytes` bound (24435 < 24576). The convention henceforth: check witness claims with native `contains`, never `printf … | grep` over interpolated output — a later ratchet may convert the older HUNK witnesses on touch. No breach, no checkpoint; no network/key/funds. **The recurring apostrophe red is closed structurally for adopting witnesses. Recommend keep-going:** bind a raw touch to the edit surface (HUNK50), convert the older scroll witnesses to native `contains`, or open a fresh Season-A / double-seat journey. |

A staged-but-undecided crossing holds its coins **reserved in transit** (spendable+reserved == start), never lost. Proven on a pledge that lapses in the suffix (Alice's coins home across the cut, vouched true on both paths; Bob too) and a far-deadline pledge honored in the suffix where **received accumulates across the base** (Bob, absent at the seal and credited in the suffix, is vouched his whole 100 inflow, not a restart). **The honest seam:** the **order-head is the whole-history fingerprint** — base included, distinct from the prefix-only head — so a party that pruned the base checks the position, not the head (`check_against` needs the whole Dag); the whole 3-block head differs from the prefix-only 2-block head, so a voucher cannot silently name a shorter order. A flipped position and a wrong signer each fail the offline verify and match no checkpoint; a corrupt Knot refuses `KnotCorrupt` before any position is read. GREEN on the first build and run; composes `voucher`+`statement`+`lapse_knot`+`lapse`+`cord`+`fold` public API only, editing none (the Voucher r1 and the Lapse-Knot re-run GREEN at the door); demo seeds — no key/funds/network/custody. A served voucher · per-account mint authority · breach renames (checkpoint-first) · deep debride · seed force-push stay the maintainer's hand. |

| `20260813.085753` | [`20260813-085753_mycelium-lapse-r1.bron`](20260813/20260813-085753_mycelium-lapse-r1.bron) | **Mycelium Lapse r1 — a reservation the order lets expire on time, decided once by the clock** — the crux of the Lapse arc (TigerBeetle's third signature primitive after the two-phase Pledge and the linked Braid): a Pledge that carries a **deadline** measured in the agreed order's own **position** — never the wall, which two nodes never share — and the ledger itself lapses it when the order's clock reaches that position, returning reserved coins to the pledger with no further signature. `mycelium/lapse.rye`, grown beside `pledge`/`cord`/`fold` and composing their public API only. Two companions sharpen order-as-clock: the **same** deadline 2 with the order walking no further stays **open** (position 2 never reached), and the same post under deadline 4 **honors**; a clean pre-deadline void releases. **Conservation unwidened** — a lapse is a void by another cause (reserved→balance), so balances + reserved == issued on every path. Refusals hold whole: `BadDeadline` (a deadline not strictly after its own opening — expired at birth), unknown pledge, wrong signer, amount mismatch, star reserve. GREEN on the first run; witness `tools/m/mycelium_lapse_witness.rish` GREEN (the Cord and the fold re-run their green binaries at the door), TAME bans clean, width-check exit 0. Refusals hold whole: out-of-sequence link (`BraidBroken`), malformed body (`Malformed`), star reserve (`StarReserve`), broken signature (`IdentityRefused`). | `20260813.060935` | [`20260813-060935_mycelium-tenure-r1.bron`](20260813/20260813-060935_mycelium-tenure-r1.bron) | **Mycelium Tenure r1 — a contested name is decided by the agreed order; the namespace earns its verdict** — the Cord commits, gossip converges, batching packs, the Knot checkpoints, and the Ledger Voice reads a lawful supply — five ways to write, agree, and read the mesh, none answering the one question a namespace exists for: when two honest keepers reach for the same name at once, who holds it? A supply total is commutative; a name is not, so the moment two keepers each sign a reservation for `bandun`, the ledger must pick one, and every honest node must pick the **same** one. Self-approved design read `active-designing/date/20260813/20260813-060935_mycelium-tenure-contested-name-exploration.md` names the four-round arc; this round seats the exploration + r1. `mycelium/tenure.rye` reads the Cord's agreed order as the namespace's **verdict** — the first reservation of a name in that order holds it, every later one loses. **The crux is a distinction `fold.fold_log` cannot draw:** it refuses the WHOLE log on `StarTaken`, which would let one node break the ledger by front-running a name; consensus exists so a front-run merely **loses**. So the Tenure walks the committed facts through `fold.fold_fact` (which returns before mutating on `StarTaken`, verified), treating a name-contest loss as a **lawful no-op** the verdict records, and re-raising `IdentityRefused`/`UnknownKind`/`Overdraw` as poison (corruption, not a lost race — `fold_fact` already checks taken-before-affordable). Proven on metal: two keepers reserve `bandun` at round 1; the agreed order grants it to exactly one — the **same** keeper across three arrival permutations (deterministic verdict, the winner computed independently in-test by the Cord's own tie-break, not hardcoded); the loser is a lawful no-op (uncontested `vega` still holds, supply folds to **800**, the loser paid nothing, the loss recorded); a block carrying a broken-fact signature refuses `IdentityRefused` and a free-name overdraw refuses `Overdraw` — corruption still poisons whole. Additive — composes `cord`+`fold` public API only, editing no elder (named per `comlink-tendency`: a name's *tenure* is the term on which it is held; grepped clean in the code namespace); demo keeper seeds — no key/funds/network/custody. A `Knot` seals a committed Cord's `fold.FoldState` (issued · taxed · every reserved star) under a streaming SHA-256 digest — no signature (a Knot is a deterministic function of the committed Dag; the digest is integrity-in-travel, never authority). `seal_dag` commits + flattens + folds + seals; `continue_verified` folds a next-epoch log onto a verified Knot. **The crux on metal:** a prefix Dag sealed to a Knot, a fresh suffix epoch continued onto it, folds to the **byte-identical supply** (issued 1000 · taxed 300 · supply 700 · one star `vega`) as the whole history from empty — checked by a deep `states_match` (issued·taxed·the whole reserved-star set, not just count). | `20260813.045139` | [`20260813-045139_mycelium-cord-batch-r2-travels.bron`](20260813/20260813-045139_mycelium-cord-batch-r2-travels.bron) | **Mycelium fact batching r2 — the batch travels as a `format cord-batch-v1` record; the agreed order crosses to a stranger as bytes** — r1 proved a batched history folds to the byte-identical supply as the same facts placed one-per-block; a consensus a developer cannot hand to another node is only a sandbox, so r2 lets a batched Dag cross as readable bytes. `mycelium/cord_batch_bron.rye` renders a committed `BatchDag` to a `format cord-batch-v1` record — per block a `block` line (author key · round · block signature), then one `fact` line per batched fact (kind · amount · star · body · signer key · fact signature), then the `parent` lines — and parses it back through a purpose-built bounded flat-Bron reader (a small pending-block state machine). **The crux (mirroring the elder cord-bron r3):** the recovered batched Dag, re-admitted under r1's own `add_batch` (every signature re-verified, every parent already held), commits to the **byte-identical order** and folds the **identical supply** (700 over one star) — two nodes agree by exchanging batched blocks alone; re-rendering the parsed record reproduces the exact bytes (a fixed point). Hit and fixed a real red in-round — a segfault (stack overflow): a `BatchDag` statically allocates `cord_max_blocks` (256) × `max_facts_per_block` (16) ≈ 1.77 MB, so returning it by value from `seat`/`rebuild` put transient whole-Dag copies on the stack; fixed structurally — both now write through an out-pointer (no return copy, the right bounded-memory move), `max_record` shrank 262144 → 16384 (a batched roster is small), and the tamper test reuses `dag2`'s storage. Refusals hold whole: a wrong header (`BadFormat`), an unknown field (`UnknownField`, never dropped), a fact before any block (`FactBeforeBlock`), an empty batch (`EmptyBatch`), and a tampered fact (`BadSignature` at `add_batch` — the batch's author signature seals every fact's signature).
